# Universal Task Ledger — Outstanding Issues, Recommendations & Tasks

Durable, cross-session repository memory of everything still outstanding: open **tasks**,
**recommendations**, **issues**, ordered execution metadata, operator decisions, provider gates, and
resolution history. Chat context is ephemeral; this file is the single universal task ledger the
[`/issues` skill](../.claude/skills/issues/SKILL.md) and session-start hook read and update.

**Rule of thumb:** if it is worth remembering after this session ends, it belongs here.

Detailed runbooks may live elsewhere, including [`operator-backlog.md`](operator-backlog.md), but
task status, priority, order, dependencies, and completion state are canonical only here.

## How this is used

- Say `/issues` in Claude Code ΓåÆ the skill reads this file and states the open items back,
  grouped by priority with a one-line summary count. Nothing is mutated on a plain read.
- `/issues add …`, `/issues done <id>`, `/issues capture`, and friends mutate the tables below.
  The full command surface lives in the skill file.
- Every mutation keeps this file committed so the memory survives across sessions and worktrees.

## Conventions

- **ID** is a monotonic `#NNN`, never reused. Allocate the next number above the current max
  across _both_ tables (open + resolved).
- **Pri**: `P1` (do next / blocking), `P2` (should do), `P3` (nice-to-have / revisit-when).
- **Type**: `task` (a concrete unit of work), `rec` (a recommendation to weigh), or
  `issue` (a defect / risk / gap).
- **Detail / next action** is the smallest thing that would move the item forward.
- **Source** points at where it came from: a doc, a PR (`#123`), a file:line, or `session YYYY-MM-DD`.
- Resolving an item moves its row to **Resolved / archive** with the date and a one-line outcome —
  rows are archived, not deleted, so the history stays auditable.

## Recommended execution queue

This queue is the ordered, prioritised view of open ledger rows — a **curated subset**, not every
open row. The detailed tables below remain the complete record; absence from this queue means
deprioritised, never closed. Revalidate a row against current
`main` before starting. Its position is not authority to call providers, spend money, change
production, commit, push, open a PR, or deploy. Acuity still decides what to start first; Optional
rows are deferred until their trigger.

Order numbers remain stable within a reconciliation snapshot. A gap means an item was completed or
removed after current-main verification; it is not missing recommended work.

- **A1 — urgent:** active safety, privacy, data-loss, or release/launch blocker. Credential
  containment, the privacy/legal package, and the Safety Plan contract are the current A1 items.
- **A2 — important:** confirmed correctness, clinical, privacy, reliability, or evaluation work.
- **A3 — planned:** worthwhile work that is safe to defer until its trigger.
- **Optional:** start only when measured need, ownership, and cost justify it.
- **Capability:** Standard = established pattern; High = cross-module senior work; Specialist =
  database/RAG/clinical/privacy expertise; Operator = named provider/product/legal authority.
- **Estimate:** focused active time, excluding approval, hosted runtime, soak, and review waits.

| #8VAY97 <!-- issue-ulid:01M0JWT29F8VAY97CG3PM5X2QW --> | P3 | task | The document_index_units retrieval path has no EXPLAIN baseline, and Phase 5 has no query-specific plan-flip evidence | REVIEW 2026-09-25 (offline audit of current main; no provider access): Still accurate: explain_retrieval_rpc accepts only four names and the _v2 functions are still in use. No measured regression, so P3. PRIOR DETAIL: Two Phase 5.1 deliverables are explicitly OPEN, not discharged. Re-graded P3 -> P2 versus the withdrawn request 2040d1fb, because that request understated the gap by claiming substitute coverage that does not exist. (A) NO EXPLAIN BASELINE FOR THE INDEX-UNITS PATH. public.explain_retrieval_rpc accepts exactly four names -- match_documents_for_query, match_document_chunks_text, match_document_lookup_chunks_text, match_document_table_facts_text -- and raises 22023 Unsupported retrieval RPC for anything else, proven against production for both match_document_chunks_text_v2 and match_document_index_units_hybrid_v2. For the first of those the v1 sibling match_document_chunks_text shares the owning table document_chunks and is a usable stand-in. For the second there is none: match_document_index_units_hybrid_v2 delegates to match_document_index_units_hybrid_scoped over document_index_units (supabase/schema.sql:8033-8054), and no supported RPC touches that table. document_index_units is one of the two section 1.2 outliers, so the outlier that most needed a baseline is the one that has none. (B) NO QUERY-SPECIFIC PLAN-FLIP EVIDENCE. explain_retrieval_rpc EXPLAINs `select * from public.<rpc>(...)`, so a PL/pgSQL body's inner plan is never exposed and every sample reports a single Function Scan with no index names. Plan section 5.1's 'record plan flips (seq scan -> index scan)' is therefore unanswerable through this instrument. The pg_stat_user_indexes read captured in Phase 5.1(c) is a WEAKER and DIFFERENT signal, not a substitute: idx_scan is cumulative across every workload touching the table and no before/after delta was captured around the samples, so it can prove an index is never chosen by anything but cannot prove that a given query changed plan. NEXT: one migration extending the explain_retrieval_rpc p_rpc branch list to the _v2 family (at minimum match_document_index_units_hybrid_v2 and match_document_chunks_text_v2), shipped in an approved window -- with D4 ON, merging it to main deploys it, so it needs the window and a green post-merge live-drift run. Then re-run npm run profile:retrieval --analyze to capture the missing baseline. For (B), consider whether an auto_explain-style capture is a better fit than widening the RPC. STOP: do not record the cumulative index-usage read as plan-flip evidence; that conflation is exactly what this row exists to prevent. | Codex review of PR #2250 (P2, comment 3833062803 and 3833062807); docs/audit/live-drift-forensics-2026-08.md Phase 5 close-out 5.1(b); supabase/schema.sql:8033-8054 | 2026-08-21 |

A uniform reading is a prompt to investigate and NOT a confirmed fault: assessDocumentIndexQuality starts the score at 1 and only subtracts penalties before rounding to three decimals, so a cleanly extracted corpus legitimately scores 1.000 for every document. Treat a uniform 0.00 as the suspicious case, since 0 is the column default and extraction_quality defaults to unknown, and corroborate against the issues array and metrics JSON on the same rows before recording anything against the scoring pipeline. | #RP00FZ <!-- issue-ulid:01M1G9RKB5RP00FZ56D7TE2A1Y --> | P2 | task | Reindex reaper: the storage-object half of the abandoned-generation cleanup is still missing | PARTIAL PROGRESS, recorded here so it is not tracked only in code comments. The DETECTION half landed on branch claude/reindex-reaper: scripts/cleanup-abandoned-reindex-generations.ts gained a read-only --alert-on-abandoned flag whose pure decision function (abandonedReindexGenerationAlertExitCode in src/lib/reindex-pipeline.ts) exits 2 when a dry run finds abandoned staged rows, and .github/workflows/reindex-reaper.yml runs it as a weekly read-only probe that opens or updates an alert issue. That workflow ships INERT (repo variable REINDEX_REAPER_ENABLED is unset) and its destructive apply path is triple-gated and statically guarded by scripts/check-github-action-pins.mjs. WHAT REMAINS, and what this row exists for: the storage-object half. cleanup_abandoned_document_index_generations deletes generation-bearing rows across seven artifact tables, but nothing enqueues storage_cleanup_jobs rows for the objects those generations left behind, so a crash mid-reindex still leaks Storage objects that no schedule revisits. The recorded fix is for the commit RPC to enqueue storage_cleanup_jobs rows for superseded generations at commit time. That is a migration, it deploys to the live clinical database on merge, and it was deliberately excluded from the detection batch. THREE PREREQUISITES the same review recorded, all of which should land before the repo variable REINDEX_REAPER_APPLY is ever set: (1) the RPC has no owner scoping and no row ceiling -- seven deletes in one transaction under a 180s statement timeout -- so a migration adding a row ceiling and a keep-most-recent-generation guard should come first; (2) the RPC's predicate makes every generation-bearing row eligible on any document whose metadata.index_generation_id is absent, so documents predating generation stamping will register as abandoned forever and the weekly probe will very likely be permanently red until either a backfill migration lands or the probe alerts on a delta rather than an absolute count; (3) arming the apply path safely needs a GitHub Environment with required reviewers on a separate apply job, because two of the three gates are standing repository variables and only client_payload.apply is per-run. Full reasoning is in the header of .github/workflows/reindex-reaper.yml. | review of branch claude/reindex-reaper, 2026-09-02 | 2026-09-02 |

No floor lowered, nothing skipped or quarantined. PROOF IT IS NOT THE APP: the twelve dark assertions in ui-caring-contacts-workspace.spec.ts PASS on Firefox in this same run (tests 48, 53, 59, 65, 72, 79, 85, 100, 138, 152, 159, 169) and every one navigates first and switches scheme after. THE WEBKIT HALF IS NOT. Six of the eight WebKit failures are phone scroll, header, footer and composer behaviour. WebKit is Safari engine, so on an iPhone those are candidate real user-visible bugs, not engine-geometry tolerance, and must be read as a bug report rather than assertion-loosening work. | #Z3GZ5P <!-- issue-ulid:01M1XS1N1YZ3GZ5PDNEACPWDY8 --> | P2 | task | DSM-5-TR specifier catalogue has no clinician sign-off: 585 items, 71 source-verified, 0 reviewed | UPDATE 2026-09-25 (offline traversal of data/specifiers-content.json; no provider access). Two corrections to this row's own headline, both reproducible from the committed file. FIRST: '71 source-verified' overstates the work. 57 of the 71 restate their own label, matching 'For <disorder>, "<label>" uses the stated threshold: <the label's own parenthesis>' — the Adjustment Disorders entry for 'Acute (less than 6 months)' resolves to 'uses the stated threshold: less than 6 months'. Only 14 carry content beyond the label, all in the ICD-11 trauma, dissociative, ARFID and personality material. Real coverage is 14 of 585, not 71. SECOND: neurodevelopmental, schizophrenia spectrum, bipolar, depressive and anxiety disorders hold 192 items and no definitions between them, so the commonest presentations are the least covered. PR #3028 made the audit recognise clinician-reviewed-approved — under the previous exact-match test the first signed specifier would have gone unexamined. | #CHPC5C <!-- issue-ulid:01M1XRS8BHCHPC5CF8NY75663V --> | P3 | issue | ui-tools differentials compare queue navigation is intermittently slow enough to time out inside the full Production UI shard 3 | UPDATE 2026-09-27: FIX BUILT on branch claude/repo-review-design-fixes-ao0dqw (PR #3145), NOT YET MERGED — close this row only after it lands. The 'way out' below was built: claimPhoneOverlayAddonReserve (use-phone-overlay-chrome-reserve.ts) is called by both portals and writes --phone-overlay-chrome-h inline as calc(<value in force> + <row>px) before settle, re-measures after settle, and subtracts the row on release. A production-build trace found a second cause: under reduced motion every element gets a 0.01ms transition and transition-property defaults to all, so the pad painted the stale padding-top for one frame; globals.css now sets transition-property: none on mobile-composer-reserve-pad at phone width. The required Playwright settle assertion was added (#CHPC5C test in tests/ui-phone-scroll.spec.ts): 10/10 pass on a production build, fails on the pre-fix portals. verify:full green (20027 tests); the full verify:phone-chrome run was stopped by the owner at ~342/687 and is left to CI. PREVIOUS: CAUSE FOUND AND PROVEN 2026-09-20; NO LONGER PARKED. This is not a flaky test — it catches a real phone defect that also hits clinicians. The Differentials mode-nav row (49px) paints IN page flow, then ModeNavHeaderPortal moves it into the fixed phone header at ~315ms; content loses that height and every element rises 49px. usePhoneOverlayChromeReserve only republishes the measured 121px at ~423ms, so for ~110ms the whole page sits 49px too high. A press that straddles that correction lands on one control and releases on another: recorded trace shows pointerdown on the compare-open link at y 492-540, pointerup at the identical y hitting "Edit selection" because the link had moved to 443-491, and the browser retargeting the click to their common ancestor. The link never activates — no error, no navigation. scrollY is 0 throughout, so this is layout, not scrolling, and Playwright hit-target checks cannot see it because press and release were each individually on a real element. EVIDENCE: 0/41 reproductions through the repo runner (10x alone, 10x under 4 busy loops, 20x under 12, plus a full shard) — shard load is not the right kind of slowness. Under Chrome DevTools renderer throttling: 0/10 at 4x, 10/10 at 10x, 9/10 at 20x, and 0/10 at 10x when the phone chrome is allowed to settle first. That last row is the control. TWO CORRECTIONS TO THIS ROW. (1) It is the PHONE click that fails, not the desktop one. tests/ui-tools.spec.ts:2928 is the test declaration; the test contains two compare-open clicks, and the URL CI recorded has no q=Pain, which matches the 390x844 phone block. Above the phone breakpoint the reserve publishes 0px and no addon row mounts, so desktop is not exposed. (2) The TO-UNBLOCK suggestion in this row does not work and is refuted: the destination "Loading presentation" role=status skeleton cannot be the settle signal, because it lives inside the destination route and only renders AFTER the URL commits. Proven by holding the destination response 20s — urlChangedAfterMs 20130, loadingSkeletonSeenAfterMs null. The "racing an unhydrated link" hypothesis is the right family but the wrong mechanism: the link is a plain next/link anchor from a Server Component, so a pre-hydration click would still navigate natively. The race is hydration-driven LAYOUT, not handlers. STILL OPEN AFTER PR #2929 — READ THIS BEFORE CLOSING THE ROW. PR #2929 (publishPhoneOverlayChromeReserveNow, called from both portals) closes this only for IN-SESSION navigation, and NOT for the cold page.goto the trace above was taken on, which is the path CI fails on. The publisher refuses to act until the reserve hook has settled a value at least once — it must, because before that responsive layout can still report the wide 200px stack (#147) and publishing that would move content twice. React runs layout effects children-first and the hook belongs to the shell, so on a cold load a portal effect ALWAYS runs before the hook has settled and the immediate publish is suppressed, every time. Established by adversarial review of #2929 and independently reproduced; pinned as failing-when-fixed cases in tests/phone-overlay-reserve-portal-wiring.dom.test.tsx, which is also the only coverage of the portals wiring at all — every case in tests/phone-overlay-chrome-reserve.dom.test.ts calls the publisher directly and would pass with both useLayoutEffect blocks deleted. A second gap: unmounting a portal does not shrink the reserve, so content sits 49px too LOW instead; a cleanup cannot simply publish, because React runs layout-effect destroys before it detaches portal children. THE WAY OUT, not yet built or verified: have the portal publish its OWN height as a delta to the reserve currently in force instead of re-measuring the whole stack. That removes the #147 exposure, and with it the settle precondition, and it gives the shrink direction naturally via the effect cleanup. It is a real change to shared phone chrome on every route and needs its own browser evidence — verify:phone-chrome plus the Playwright settle assertion docs/search-chrome-behaviour.md mandates, which was not added and is still required. OWNER DECISION PENDING on whether to do that work. MERGE VERIFICATION, carried over from the refuted request a13b357d so it is not lost: #2929 merged 2026-09-20 as commit 80cf3df6c69a, and publishPhoneOverlayChromeReserveNow is present on origin/main in globals.css, phone-header-collapse-portal.tsx and use-phone-overlay-chrome-reserve.ts. That request read presence as proof the defect was fixed and told future sessions it MUST NOT BE REDONE; presence is not execution, and the scope limit above is why. It was cancelled with that reasoning recorded. | Claude Code session 2026-09-20 (session_01VE1C4eJidBwMdfHV4nUJgL); reissued against the current row after fbc169da applied; scope limit reproduced in tests/phone-overlay-reserve-portal-wiring.dom.test.tsx (PR #2938) | 2026-09-07 |

| #81A3YJ <!-- issue-ulid:01M2MS3DGS81A3YJQTS5FQ6G9Y --> | P2 | issue | Drift def_hash normaliser strips from any '--' to end of line, hiding 91 percent of the P03 seed function | schema_drift_snapshot() computes a function def_hash as md5 of pg_get_functiondef with block comments, then '--[^\n]*', then all whitespace removed (supabase/migrations/20260706200000_schema_drift_snapshot.sql:89 and the 20260818090000 history-probe rewrite:97). The line-comment regex cannot tell a real comment from '--' inside a string literal. public.site_content_registry_baseline embeds the whole registry seed as a single-line dollar-quoted JSON blob, and the first '--' falls at offset 176107 inside a service URL (Next-Step---Drug-and-Alcohol-Services), so 91.1 percent of the definition - 1841007 normalised chars down to 164524 - is stripped before hashing. Measured, not inferred: replaying both the old and the new function body into a local Postgres 16 produced the identical pinned hash c2dc2183657bab3c994af7f18ddfcb9b even though the bodies differ, and that same replay reproduces the manifest value exactly for the unchanged body, so the control is sound. Consequence: check:drift and the post-merge live-drift workflow cannot see a change to that function's seed data after the first '--'. Every other function loses only genuine comments, which is the intended behaviour. A fix has to distinguish a comment from a literal - dollar-quoted bodies could be hashed unstripped, or the strip applied only outside string literals - and it changes def_hash for every function, so it needs a manifest regeneration in the same change. | scripts/refresh-site-content-p03-baseline.ts | 2026-09-16 |
| #TNYENC <!-- issue-ulid:01M2NAGY09TNYENCKDDYX9F268 --> | P3 | issue | site_content_registry_baseline on live holds only the 281 frozen baselines, so a record published after 2026-08-24 has no baseline for SQL field merging | REVIEW 2026-09-25 (offline audit of current main; no provider access): No forward migration for site_content_registry_baseline exists after 20260824122000. Matters only once #HTZPQ8 and #ZPHK2F clear, so P3. Under #81A3YJ a seed change here would be invisible to check:drift after the first '--', so do #81A3YJ first. PRIOR DETAIL: site_content_registry_baseline(kind, slug) is an immutable SQL lookup whose blob was frozen into migration 20260824122000 with 281 entries. site_content_registry_source_render calls it for every registry record it renders, so a service or form published after the freeze resolves to a null baseline and loses the forced-field and null merging the frozen records get. PR #2814 tried to fix this by regenerating the applied migration, which cannot reach live and was reverted on 2026-09-16. THE CORRECT MECHANISM is a NEW forward migration carrying create or replace function public.site_content_registry_baseline with the current catalogue blob: the Supabase integration applies new versions, so it reaches live, and it changes only an immutable lookup body with no data write and no lock. It must merge inside an approved Supabase window per AGENTS.md, and it is only worth doing when the publication pipeline is actually going to publish post-freeze records, so it is coupled to the P1 row about the live catalogue still serving the 2026-08-24 freeze. | Session 2026-09-16 repairing the epoch-zero freeze regression from PR #2814 | 2026-09-16 |

| #4W704K <!-- issue-ulid:01M2P2QFGQ4W704K34MD30SX6G --> | P3 | issue | site_content_release_records_immutable is not 'enable always', so session_replication_role = replica bypasses the freeze's only structural protection | REVIEW 2026-09-25 (offline audit of current main; no provider access): Two of three gaps closed by 20260916190000_seal_bootstrap_release_and_drop_per_read_digest.sql (#2855: enable-always insert/truncate triggers, per-read digest removed). Remaining: the one-line enable-always promotion of site_content_release_records_immutable, deliberately left out of that migration. The sequencing advice is moot. PRIOR DETAIL: READ FROM THE SCHEMA ON 2026-09-16, NOT EXPLOITED OR TESTED. supabase/schema.sql:12722-12724 creates site_content_release_records_immutable as an ordinary row trigger (before update or delete ... execute function guard_site_content_immutable_row). PostgreSQL does not fire an ordinary trigger when session_replication_role = 'replica', so a session that sets that GUC can UPDATE or DELETE rows of the frozen epoch-zero release with nothing raising. That is not a theoretical technique in this repository: scripts/check-site-content-control-plane.mjs:889-894 uses exactly it against a sibling table. TWO FURTHER GAPS IN THE SAME SEAL: the trigger covers only UPDATE and DELETE, so nothing prevents a row being INSERTED into a retained bootstrap release (it need only satisfy site_content_release_records_check1), and TRUNCATE is a statement-level event a row trigger never sees. Until the catalogue read-latency migration lands, the per-read bootstrap digest recomputation is the only thing that detects an inserted row, and it detects it after the fact — see docs/audit/2026-09-16-catalogue-read-latency.md. HOW MUCH THIS MATTERS, stated honestly: setting session_replication_role needs the same superuser or postgres privilege an attacker would need to write the rows anyway, and such an attacker could equally replace the digest function. So the value here is against ACCIDENT — a half-applied migration, a bad replay, a partial restore, or a script that sets the GUC for an unrelated reason and then touches this table — not against a determined adversary. THE FIX: 'alter table public.site_content_release_records enable always trigger site_content_release_records_immutable', plus an 'enable always' before-insert trigger restricted to the retained bootstrap release ids and an 'enable always' before-truncate statement trigger. All transactional, no index build. SEQUENCE IT WITH the read-latency migration, which needs the same seal to justify removing the per-read digest; doing it separately means doing the same CREATE TRIGGER lock twice. GATE: SQL, so it ships as a new forward migration and needs an approved merge window. RUN check:site-content-control-plane in Docker FIRST — its own fixtures rely on session_replication_role on a sibling table, and an 'enable always' trigger on this one must be proven not to break them before merging. | docs/audit/2026-09-16-catalogue-read-latency.md; Claude Code session 2026-09-16 (session_01GJHMbC4s2S2n8aChAxVF1D), catalogue read-latency investigation | 2026-09-16 |

| #GQR7DE <!-- issue-ulid:01M2P2Q02BGQR7DE5Z48476ZKN --> | P3 | issue | site_content_canonical_json is recursive plpgsql, so release activation will slow the same way the read path did once a release exceeds a couple of thousand records | REVIEW 2026-09-25 (offline audit of current main; no provider access): The row's own action is 'nothing yet', so P3. site_content_canonical_json is untouched. PRIOR DETAIL: READ FROM THE SQL ON 2026-09-16, NOT MEASURED. site_content_canonical_json (supabase/schema.sql:12746) is a recursive plpgsql function: one invocation per JSON node, and a string_agg subquery over jsonb_each or jsonb_array_elements at every container. It defines EVERY fingerprint in the site-content control plane — release digest, contentHash, publicationVersion, governance and lineage hashes, plan and receipt identities — and is reimplemented in JS and pinned by tests/site-content-p03-baseline.test.ts, so it cannot be rewritten without invalidating stored digests across the live database. Measured proxy for its cost: the 843-record epoch-zero freeze canonicalises to 9,522,923 bytes across 164,820 JSON nodes (55,103 containers), i.e. ~165k plpgsql invocations and ~55k planned aggregate subqueries per pass, and two such passes per HTTP request cost about four seconds against live — see docs/audit/2026-09-16-catalogue-read-latency.md. The read path is being fixed by removing the calls. The WRITE path cannot be: site_content_dynamic_state_digest, site_content_release_digest, stage_site_content_sync_event, activate_site_content_release and rollback_site_content_release all canonicalise the full release by design and must keep doing so. Today that is tolerable because activation is rare and operator-driven. It scales linearly in node count, so a release of 2,000+ records makes activation slow in exactly the same way, and an activation that exceeds its statement timeout fails a publication rather than a page view. WHAT TO DO: nothing yet — this is a recorded scaling limit, not a defect. BEFORE the catalogue grows materially past the freeze, time one activation against a realistic release on a local Postgres (npm run check:site-content-control-plane already replays the control plane in Docker) and record the node-count-to-seconds curve, so the ceiling is known before an operator hits it mid-publication. DO NOT rewrite site_content_canonical_json to a set-based or C-backed equivalent as a performance fix: any byte-level difference changes every stored fingerprint, and a C extension cannot be installed by migration on Supabase. | docs/audit/2026-09-16-catalogue-read-latency.md; Claude Code session 2026-09-16 (session_01GJHMbC4s2S2n8aChAxVF1D), catalogue read-latency investigation | 2026-09-16 |

| #TDCZJB <!-- issue-ulid:01M2ZM3WWQTDCZJB9SBM57XFMK --> | P1 | issue | Ward Flow's synthetic patient records are in 1629 published commits under a 2026-08-31 ruling the owner has deferred, not withdrawn | UPDATE 2026-09-26 (removal PR, branch claude/remove-ward-flow-caring-contacts-ltxdgk; offline, no provider access): The owner retired Ward Flow on 2026-09-26 and its files, including the synthetic patient seed, are deleted from main by the removal PR. That does NOT change this item: every earlier commit still carries them in the public history. The owner rulings this row cites were deleted with the feature; read them at commit 8ec68fd32 (docs/ward-flow-owner-rulings-2026-08-31.md s5 and docs/ward-flow-owner-rulings-2026-08-31-community.md item 9). The adversarial review also found one more invented patient outside the UM10000 series: 'D. Okonkwo, UMRN 2214887' in docs/ward-flow/design/prototypes/mockup-referral.html (at 8ec68fd32). Options remain: make the repo private, or publish a fresh history. PRIOR DETAIL: REFRAMED 2026-09-20 after Codex correctly flagged the original wording on PR #2930, and RE-MEASURED the same day after a second Codex review found the exposure figures understated three times running. The original row was titled around the repository contradicting the 2026-09-01 assume-private ruling. THAT CONTRADICTION IS RESOLVED and must not be re-raised: PR #2927 superseded that ruling in docs/ward-flow-owner-rulings-2026-09-01.md with the owner's 2026-09-20 decision, quoted verbatim - 'i will leave it public for now' - and recorded the first actual measurement (GitHub API: private=false, visibility=public). Leaving the row as written would have taught later sessions to treat a deliberate owner decision as an urgent unresolved defect, which is the same absence-read-as-signal failure this ledger exists to prevent. WHAT IS ACTUALLY OPEN is the 2026-08-31 ruling, section 5: 'Ward Flow is NOT to be pushed to a public repository as it stands.' The owner has DEFERRED that decision (2026-08-31 community audit, item 9: 'Defer for now - his decision, made knowing the eight invented patient records and three commits of history are public'), and deferred is not withdrawn. THE MEASURED BASIS IS FAR LARGER THAN THE RULING RECORDED. Files, pinned at commit 8d5cea0d1: the UMRNs are in 16 files where the ruling recorded 2, of which 14 CARRY the data and 2 only discuss it, and the names in 8 where it recorded 5. Commits: 1629 published commits carry the records, 1335 of them on origin/main - not the 3 the ruling recorded and every later restatement repeated. THE COMMIT FIGURE IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS, because it is what the ruling's own argument rests on: 'publishing a repository publishes its history'. Every earlier count used git log -S, the pickaxe, which finds commits where a string's PRESENCE CHANGES, not commits whose TREE CONTAINS it. There are only two distinct versions of src/components/ward-management/ward-patients-seed.ts in the entire repository and BOTH hold the eight records, so every commit carrying that file exposes them. Verified by enumerating the blob at that path across every commit reachable from the origin refs. WHY THE FILE FIGURE IS PINNED TO A SHA: every document that discusses this exposure - the two ruling files, this row, the inbox requests behind it - must name UM10000 or Talia Halloway to discuss it at all, and so joins the count. At the head of the commit that introduced the corrected paragraph the same commands already returned 20 and 12. Quote the pinned figure, never a fresh HEAD count, or each retelling makes the exposure look worse while nothing has changed. THREE SUCCESSIVE MEASUREMENTS RAN LOW, ALL IN THE SAME DIRECTION: 12 files / 4 commits, then 16 files / 3 commits, then this. The 12 came from searching UM100001, the first record of eight, missing four files carrying only one of the other seven. A privacy count is easy to take against one specimen, one branch, or one kind of change, and every such shortcut truncates - toward making publication look safer. Measure containment, not change, and pin the SHA. The records themselves are genuinely synthetic - the file header reasons the choice at length and the screens carry a visible synthetic-data marker - and the ruling's point was never that they are real, but that they do not look invented: eight full names, each with a UMRN and a date of birth, in a psychiatrist's public repository. The two options the owner approved at the time remain the options, and the containment count is the argument for the second: make the repository private, or replace every name and UMRN wherever they appear and publish a FRESH repository with a single initial commit, since rewriting 1629 commits of history is not a realistic alternative. NEXT ACTION IS THE OWNER'S, not an assistant's: this row exists to keep the deferral visible, not to reopen a settled decision. | Codex reviews on PR #2930 and #2934; owner decision 2026-09-20; docs/ward-flow-owner-rulings-2026-08-31.md s5, -2026-08-31-community.md item 9; containment count and pinned file count measured 2026-09-20 | 2026-09-20 |

| #JMX32A <!-- issue-ulid:01M2ZNXXABJMX32ASS4ANN0W5P --> | P2 | issue | A Playwright run whose own web server fails to start silently reuses another server and reports green | Observed 2026-09-20 while writing tests/ui-patient-number-field.spec.ts. The agent running scripts/run-playwright.mjs had its own webServer fail to start because a concurrent session already held the port; the runner did not stop. It proceeded against THAT other server and reported a green pass. The run was re-done cleanly afterwards and the reported result is sound, but the hazard is not the individual run — it is that a browser gate can report success without ever exercising the tree it claims to test. This is the same class of failure as a gate piped into tail (exit code masked) and as CLINICAL_HAZARD_CONTROLS_PASS printing while a named proof file had been emptied: a signal that cannot fail is worse than no signal, because it is trusted. It matters more here than in most repos because verification evidence is quoted verbatim into PR bodies and the ledger, so a contaminated run propagates as fact. NEXT: make Playwright webServer startup failure fatal rather than falling back to an already-listening port — either set reuseExistingServer:false for CI/agent runs, or have run-playwright.mjs confirm the server it connects to is the one it started (the repo already has /api/local-project-id for exactly this identity question, used by npm run ensure). Add a contract test that a failed server start exits non-zero. Note npm run ensure already solves the adjacent problem correctly and is the pattern to copy. | Claude Code session 2026-09-20 (session_01VE1C4eJidBwMdfHV4nUJgL); reported by the agent that wrote the #WFARS3 browser guard | 2026-09-20 |
| #33346A <!-- issue-ulid:01M2ZTPSXQ33346AYMPF41R5AR --> | P2 | issue | Two Firefox failures survive the 2026-09-20 triage: the document-viewer hit counter and the tools Forms panel, both a click whose state change never lands | Read the Playwright trace from the failing run BEFORE proposing any fix; playwright.config.ts sets trace: retain-on-failure so it exists, and its After-DOM snapshot of the click answers this in minutes. THE TWO: (1) ui-smoke.spec.ts "search regressions avoid fetch errors and open viewer hits @critical" - after clicking Next document search hit, "Hit 2 of 2" never appears (element NOT FOUND, not hidden); (2) ui-tools.spec.ts "tool links point to the expected in-app modes" - the Forms complementary panel resolves to zero elements, on the 4th of 6 loop passes, while Services with an identical /?mode=<id> href passes. SAME SHAPE, so probably one shared Firefox cause. RULED OUT by two independent agent reads, do not re-derive: text split across elements (Playwright elementText flattens descendants and JSX/SSR comment separators); a collapsed <details> or any CSS cause (a hidden node still matches, and would report hidden not not-found - no stylesheet removes a node from the DOM); scrollIntoView/smooth scroll (suite-wide reducedMotion: reduce makes resolveScrollBehavior return auto); keyboard/focus; a click intercepted by the fixed composer (Playwright's hit-target interceptor blocks and retries, failing AT the click); Forms-specific data or markup (tools-catalog.ts Forms and Services are the same shape, and DetailActions renders the href link unconditionally). LEADING CANDIDATES: for (1), the handler firing zero times or twice - with exactly 2 hits, (i+d+2)%2 returns to 0 either way - with the exclusive-accordion open-attribute fight at source-panels.tsx:1063 as a plausible Gecko-specific trigger; for (2), the panel's hardcoded aria-labelledby id resolving against a hidden duplicate page root (the #093 failure mode visibleByTestId exists for), or the bare focus() at tools-search-results-page.tsx:470 scrolling late. NEITHER IS PROVEN. Why: Why it matters - if the click genuinely no-ops, a Firefox user cannot page through document search hits at all, which is a clinical reading path; if it double-fires it is a harness artefact and the test should assert the active chunk id rather than a modulo-2 counter. Context: Six Firefox failures in main run 35503893104; four fixed in PRs #2932 and #2933, these two left. Parent row #9Z197J. Owner: unknown - depends on what the trace shows. Confidence: high that the cause is shared and not CSS/text-matching; low on any single mechanism. Depends on: the egress row - the trace could not be downloaded from the cloud container. | session 2026-09-20 Firefox failure triage; two parallel agent investigations; main run 35503893104 | 2026-09-20 |

THE TWO DANGEROUS CONSEQUENCES ARE ALREADY CLOSED and must not be re-reported: the row is not published, because fetchSharedOnCallEntries filters on the RAW row via rowMayBeComplianceRequirement, which fails closed on unreadable details; and a later save no longer destroys the stored details, because of salvageExistingDetails in src/lib/on-call/editor-details.ts. What remains is the misfiling. Closing it means either carrying the raw details alongside the parsed ones on OnCallEntry, or giving isComplianceEntry the raw-row reading that rowMayBeComplianceRequirement already has. Either way a type that every consumer in the mode reads changes, and it changes for a row no editor in this app can produce, because the API validates against the same strict schema on write -- reachable only by a direct database edit, an import, or a row written before the current write path. Deferred on that cost against that benefit. NEXT ACTION: decide whether OnCallEntry should carry raw details at all; if the On Call view refactor lands first, make the decision there rather than as a separate type change. | Session 2026-09-20, On Call Compliance page clinical-governance review | 2026-09-20 |

| #PM71Z1 <!-- issue-ulid:01M2ZTQ884PM71Z1546JP1SMVH --> | P2 | task | Three blocked hostnames stop any cloud session running Firefox/WebKit or reading a CI trace, so browser failures can only be reasoned about from logs | Add cdn.playwright.dev, playwright.download.prss.microsoft.com and *.blob.core.windows.net to the Claude Cloud environment's egress allowlist; owner action in the environment settings, no repo change. THE THREE, each verified by a real request, not inferred: the first two are the ONLY mirrors in playwright-core 1.63.0's PLAYWRIGHT_CDN_MIRRORS (the old *.azureedge.net entries were removed in this version, so allowlisting those achieves nothing) and both return 403 request blocked; the third serves GitHub Actions artifacts, and an artifact download redirects there and returns CONNECT tunnel failed 403, which is why a retained Playwright trace cannot be fetched. ALSO CHECKED AND DEAD: ftp/archive/download.mozilla.org, snapcraft, flathub, the mozillateam PPA, codeload, and the npm @playwright/browser-firefox package (a 15KB stub that downloads from the same blocked CDN). A stock apt/tarball Firefox is NOT a substitute at any version - Playwright launches with -juggler-pipe and waits for "Juggler listening to the pipe", which only its own patched build emits - and there is no channel: firefox and no PLAYWRIGHT_FIREFOX_EXECUTABLE_PATH; the repo's PLAYWRIGHT_CHROMIUM_EXECUTABLE_PATH is its own invention and Chromium-only. Why: This is the constraint that shaped the whole 2026-09-20 triage. Four Firefox failures were fixed without a single Firefox run, and the dark-mode one was nearly written up as a broken app for every Firefox and Safari user before a cross-check refuted it; the two that remain are stuck precisely because the trace that would settle them is behind the third hostname. Context: Also worth knowing separately - the preinstalled Chromium is 141 (revision 1194) where playwright-core 1.63.0 pins revision 1243 / Chrome 153, so even local Chromium proof is twelve majors behind CI. Owner: Josh - environment settings. Confidence: high; every claim above was checked by running the request or reading node_modules/playwright-core. Depends on: nothing. | session 2026-09-20; agent investigation of browser provisioning; proxy README /root/.ccr/README.md | 2026-09-20 |

| #1RWC0H <!-- issue-ulid:01M2ZP1HGA1RWC0H65TK64BMP7 --> | P2 | issue | Caller IP addresses are stored as unsalted truncated SHA-256 with no retention limit in api_rate_limit_subjects | anonymousApiSubjectKey (src/lib/public-api-access.ts) derives the rate-limit subject as the prefix anon: plus the first 32 hex characters of an unsalted SHA-256 of the trusted proxy IP, and stores it in api_rate_limit_subjects.subject_key. The IPv4 address space is small enough that an unsalted hash of it is brute-forced in seconds, so these rows are PSEUDONYMISED, not anonymised, and should be classified as personal information rather than treated as opaque. Separately, and unlike rag_queries, the table has no purge function and no retention window, so rows persist indefinitely (supabase/migrations/20260704000000_api_rate_limit_subjects.sql enables row level security and grants select/insert/update/delete to service_role only). NOT AN EXPOSURE: RLS is on and only service_role reaches it, so a stranger cannot read it. This is a retention and classification matter, which is why it was deferred rather than fixed on the day. NEXT ACTION: two separable decisions. (a) Replace the bare hash with a keyed HMAC using an environment secret, so the key stops being a reversible IP -- note that rotating that secret resets every live rate-limit window, so it needs a deliberate moment. (b) Ship a retention purge mirroring the rag_queries retention migration (supabase/migrations/20260629060603_rag_queries_retention.sql is the pattern). Both are Supabase migrations, so both are owner-merged and reach the live clinical database within seconds of merge. | Session 2026-09-20, On Call Compliance page security audit | 2026-09-20 |

| #ZPHK2F <!-- issue-ulid:01M2ZWB0JAZPHK2FRCYN41VQM9 --> | P1 | issue | Owner design decision: first publication versus adoption for the site catalogue (blocks #HTZPQ8) | OWNER DECISION 2026-09-25: first publication, not adoption. The 222 owner-scoped service rows and 54 forms go public through the administrator first-publication flow in owner-approved batches, each batch clinically signed off (the 17 newest service records need sign-off before they can publish). Do not relax the reconciliation triage to make adoption pass. Next action: design and build the first-publication path; it touches site_content tables, so any migration follows the Supabase merge-is-deploy rule. PRIOR DETAIL: REVIEW 2026-09-25 (offline audit of current main; no provider access): The 'smaller, separate' part is fixed: a27fc99a9 adds a not_comparable verdict with an honest message and seven tests, and docs/site-content-publication-handover.md section 2.5 warns that steps 2-3 cannot complete. What remains is the owner decision between first publication and adoption. PRIOR DETAIL: WHAT HAPPENS. scripts/build-site-content-reconciliation-plan.ts, run against a fresh operator export (843 records, activeReleaseId e4a1dd29, initialized=false, expected=actual=843), reports: owner rows 222, logical groups 222, published groups 843, groups not published 3, TRIAGE SNAPSHOTS 0, needs owner review 222, adoptable 0, identical duplicates 0. It then refuses, telling the operator that 222 groups diverge from the published snapshot and that choosing between them is a clinical judgement. WHY THAT MESSAGE IS MISLEADING. Nothing diverged. buildRegistryReconciliationReport can only mark a group adoptable when canonicalCandidate returns a candidate, and canonicalCandidate delegates to reconcileCanonicalPublicSiteContent, which requires ALL FOUR of publicationState='published', renderedByPublicSite=true, explicitlyReconciled=true and rowOwnerId=null. Measured on live: clinical_registry_records has 222 service rows and 54 form rows, ALL with a non-null owner_id; site_content_publications has 0 rows; site_content_public_records has 0 rows. So no candidate can satisfy those conditions in this state, no triage snapshot is ever built, and every group falls through to divergent_requires_administrator_review by construction. The count 222-of-222 is an artefact of the state, not a measurement of drift. WHY IT MATTERS. The handover (docs/site-content-publication-handover.md) tells the owner the order is export, then reconciliation plan, then the administrator records it. Step 2 cannot produce output, so the owner would follow the documented order and hit a refusal that blames the corpus. Worse, the refusal is the kind that invites someone to 'fix' it by relaxing the triage, which would mark owner-scoped rows canonical for a clinical publication - exactly the judgement the script exists to refuse. WHAT IS ACTUALLY NEEDED, and it is a design decision rather than a bug fix. The reconciliation/adoption path is built for rows that are ALREADY public and whose identity is settled; it adopts them into the control plane. This corpus has never published anything through the control plane at all - the epoch-zero freeze lives only in site_content_release_records. So the question to settle before any more tooling is whether initial adoption is the right mechanism here, or whether these 222 owner-scoped service rows need a first-publication path that makes them public and unowned through the administrator flow. Do NOT resolve it by loosening reconcileCanonicalPublicSiteContent. SMALLER, SEPARATE: the planner should say what it actually found. A refusal that reports 'triage snapshots 0' alongside 'needs owner review 222' should state that the two together mean nothing was comparable, not that 222 things disagreed. | live read 2026-09-21 of clinical_registry_records, site_content_publications and site_content_public_records, plus a run of scripts/build-site-content-reconciliation-plan.ts against the real export | 2026-09-20 |
| #WR6FAW <!-- issue-ulid:01M2ZRFAJRWR6FAWA79TE566QG --> | P2 | issue | verify:phone-chrome and verify:ui cannot go green in a cloud sandbox: three failures are the unpinned Chromium, not the code | Established 2026-09-20 by running the three failures against a clean origin/main worktree (aef181f55) with the same environment, and reading the saved failure snapshots. They reproduce identically on main, so they are a property of the sandbox, not of any branch. ROOT CAUSE, found rather than guessed: the only installable browser here is /opt/pw-browsers/chromium = chromium-1194 = Chromium 141.0.7390.37, because cdn.playwright.dev is 403 under the egress policy and the revision Playwright 1.63.0 pins is 1243 / Chromium 153. (1) tests/ui-smoke.spec.ts "document frame stretches the canvas owner at phone and desktop" and "document viewer puts the PDF preview first with pinned evidence after it on mobile" both fail with getByTestId(pdf-canvas-scroll).locator(canvas) "element(s) not found". The error-context snapshot shows the viewer in its error state with the text "this[#re].getOrInsertComputed is not a function" and a Retry preview button. pdfjs-dist@6.3.289 calls Map.prototype.getOrInsertComputed, a V8 addition Chromium 141 does not have, so pdf.js throws during load and no canvas is ever created. The demo PDFs and cover images are all present; nothing is missing from the fixture. (2) tests/ui-pwa.spec.ts "has a browser-valid manifest, installable icons, and a root worker" fails with installability error "in-incognito" — Chromium refusing because every Playwright BrowserContext is off-the-record. Every other assertion in that test (manifest URL, manifest contents, all icon probes, service-worker scope/state/controller) passes first. CONSEQUENCE FOR AGENTS: three failures out of ~798 passing is the FLOOR for a chromium browser gate in this environment, on any branch. Do not read it as a branch signal, and do not "fix" a test to make it pass here. CI runs the pinned browser and the Production UI chromium shards are green on recent main (7936c3f9, cfb5ed90, and shard 3 on aef181f55). NOT ESTABLISHED: per-test CI granularity, because gh run view --log is Forbidden here (results-receiver.actions.githubusercontent.com); only job-level green was read. That Chromium 153 stops reporting in-incognito is an inference from CI being green, not a direct observation. TO UNBLOCK: either allowlist cdn.playwright.dev so the pinned revision installs, or teach the browser gate to recognise this known trio and report them as environment-floor rather than failures. | Claude Code session 2026-09-20 (session_01VE1C4eJidBwMdfHV4nUJgL); reproduced on a clean origin/main worktree with the same runner and environment | 2026-09-20 |
| #NB4SHF <!-- issue-ulid:01M2ZP1H24NB4SHF2FYWGPMQEW --> | P2 | rec | On Call keeps deciding labels, glyphs, destinations and counts from entry.section, so every view over a section is misrendered -- move the view concept into src/lib | REVIEW 2026-09-25 (offline audit of current main; no provider access): Not done, but the path is stale: onCallViewForEntry and onCallEntryHref moved to src/components/on-call/on-call-entry-view.ts (276d3ef5e). OnCallPageView and the VIEW/SECTION maps remain in on-call-section-identity.ts, with 29 references to the four SECTION maps outside it. PR #3008 added no new section-keyed labels. Lowered to P2: the known symptoms are fixed and this is prevention of a bug class. PRIOR DETAIL: The Compliance page is a VIEW over the stored logistics section discriminated by details.kind, not a seventh section: section is a database CHECK constraint and merging a migration reaches the live clinical database within seconds. Any code that reads entry.section to choose a label, icon, destination or count therefore answers Admin for a compliance row. A 2026-09-20 sweep found and fixed seven such sites, then found seven more; all fourteen are fixed, but the cause is not, and this bug class will keep recurring as the mode grows. Two structural reasons, and both are the point of this row. (a) The type system endorses it: ON_CALL_SECTION_HREFS[entry.section] compiles and is wrong, and there is no lint rule against it. (b) The correct helper onCallViewForEntry lives in src/components/on-call/on-call-section-identity.ts, and tests/lib-layering.test.ts forbids src/lib from importing src/components -- so src/lib/on-call/freshness-summary.ts, src/lib/on-call/entry-search.ts and src/lib/on-call/entry-chips.ts are section-keyed because they CANNOT call it, not because anyone chose that. NEXT ACTION, in order of leverage: (1) move the view concept (OnCallPageView, ON_CALL_VIEW_TITLES/ICONS/HREFS, onCallViewForEntry, onCallViewStorageSection) out of src/components/on-call/on-call-section-identity.ts into a new src/lib/on-call/view.ts, which dissolves the layering block that created half the defects; (2) derive a non-stored view field on the entry at the one read boundary, rowToOnCallEntry in src/lib/on-call/repository.ts, so consumers read entry.view and the fix costs no migration and no database change; (3) stop exporting the section-keyed maps (ON_CALL_SECTION_TITLES, ON_CALL_SECTION_ICONS, ON_CALL_SECTION_TILE_DESCRIPTIONS, ON_CALL_SECTION_HREFS) so the wrong answer stops compiling. Deferred on 2026-09-20 because it changes every consumer in the mode and the fourteen live symptoms were already fixed. Unblocks the summariseOnCallFreshness row. Scope check before starting: grep entry.section and .section] across src/lib/on-call and src/components/on-call. | Session 2026-09-20, On Call Compliance page clinical-governance review | 2026-09-20 |

| #GQG3NV <!-- issue-ulid:01M33P8T9XGQG3NV31WKK54QDP --> | P2 | issue | vitest run silently ignores a named-but-missing test file and exits 0, so a gate receipt can name tests that never ran | MEASURED 2026-09-21. 'npx vitest run tests/this-file-does-not-exist.test.ts tests/owner-scope.test.ts' reports 'Tests 9 passed (9)' and exits 0. Two files named, one real, no warning, no non-zero exit. HOW IT BIT: on PR #2974 a commit message claimed 'the committed test that checks both surfaces agree needed no change - verified rather than assumed, by running it', citing 'npx vitest run tests/tenancy-scan.test.ts tests/owner-scope*.test.ts -> Test Files 2 passed, Tests 19 passed'. tests/tenancy-scan.test.ts DOES NOT EXIST; the paired test is tests/api-tenancy-predicates.test.ts and was never loaded. The 19 tests came from owner-scope-guard and owner-scope alone. The conclusion happened to be correct (verified afterwards: api-tenancy-predicates passes with and without the doc row) but the evidence sentence was false, and in this repository gate receipts are quoted verbatim into PR bodies and the review ledger, so a false one propagates as fact. WHY IT IS P2 AND NOT COSMETIC: this is the same class as #JMX32A (a Playwright run whose own web server failed to start silently reused another and reported green) - a signal that cannot fail is worse than no signal, because it is trusted. It is also the class docs/agents/verification-gates.md exists to protect, and AGENTS.md 'Evidence and calibration are never compressed' requires pasting the decisive gate line, which this makes unsafe to trust. NEXT ACTION: 'npm run test:focused -- --files <paths>' is already documented as failing closed for deleted files; the bare 'vitest run <paths>' path has no such guard. Either route agents to the focused wrapper for named-file runs, or add a preflight that errors when a named spec path does not exist. Add a contract test that a named-but-missing spec exits non-zero. Not a RAG, clinical or privacy surface; offline gate only. | PR #2974, 2026-09-21 — measured directly after a false evidence line in commit 6271ca1b3; correction posted at https://github.com/BigSimmo/Database/pull/2974#issuecomment-5763434640 | 2026-09-22 |

| #MSJT12 <!-- issue-ulid:01M34HDQQKMSJT12GKQ48AM3HF --> | P1 | issue | Production deploys stopped landing for 27 hours on 2026-09-21; root cause unknown and GitHub-issue monitor routing still did not drive action | Second occurrence of the Railway deploy-rollback failure mode first seen 2026-09-11..09-14 (24 consecutive rollbacks, documented in scripts/check-live-deployment-freshness.mjs and docs/deployment-architecture.md 'Readiness: what /api/health/ready may and may not ask'). Measured 2026-09-22 from GitHub deployment statuses: the Database service in the production environment last landed a deploy at 2026-09-21T05:59Z (3a43f808d) and did not land another until 2026-09-22T09:15Z (76bf29ffa, PR #2978). In between, four production deployments were created for ef257fa5c, f44ae5397, 56b123509 and 373046b76 and every one went in_progress -> inactive without ever reaching success. All four built cleanly in the Docker image build workflow, live-drift was green on the 2026-09-21 migrations, and Railway built PR preview environments normally throughout, so the integration, the image and the database are all ruled out. That leaves Railway-side deploy/start/healthcheck rejection, which is the documented mechanism: each healthcheck attempt gets ~10s against /api/health/ready and a container that cannot answer inside the window is discarded and rolled back, leaving the previous deploy serving. Confirming which of the four failed and why needs the Railway deployment logs for the production environment, which no agent session has had access to. Production recovered on its own and no fix was applied, so the trigger is unknown and can recur. Separately confirmed NOT a fault: nine main commits in the same window got a staging deploy but no production deploy at all - all nine touched no path matched by watchPatterns in railway.app.json, which is correct filtering. Alerting clarification (rewrites a false claim in the original draft of this request): live-domain-monitor.yml already has monitor-routing (issues: write + actions/github-script) as of #2884 / ledger #TN512M, and it DID fire during this stall — pinned issue #2973 ('Live domain monitor failing', label live-monitor-failure) was opened 2026-09-21T12:40Z, updated on later red runs including the deployment-freshness failure at 2026-09-22T06:47Z, and closed on the next green run at 2026-09-22T12:41Z. So 'nothing routes a red monitor run' is false. What remains unverified for human delivery: despite #2973 being open and repeatedly commented for ~24 hours of the stall, the gap was still found only because a human inspected GitHub deployment statuses, not because anyone acted on the pinned issue. That matches the #TN512M residual ('Close once a genuine outage has produced a notification someone acted on') and the workflow comment that owner failure emails arrive unread. Durable next actions: (1) pull Railway production deployment logs for the four inactive deploys and record the reject reason; (2) treat this stall as the missing #TN512M real-outage evidence that GitHub-issue routing alone is insufficient delivery, and decide a human-reachable path (page/Slack/owner push) before adding more probes. Pairs with #HYSWAF (Railway deploys every main push without waiting for required checks) and #TN512M. | Claude session 2026-09-22; GitHub deployment statuses for Database/production; live-domain-monitor runs 35639421619/35696377977/35728663173 and issue #2973 routing evidence; Codex review on PR #2982 | 2026-09-22 |

| #F9HZEG <!-- issue-ulid:01M3CHC1JDF9HZEG738N2MBN2W --> | P1 | task | CME and On Call have no two-user staging isolation proof; the cross-tenant harness covers documents/search/answer only (audit F17) | UPDATE 2026-09-26 (branch claude/cross-tenant-cme-oncall-writes, offline; supersedes the same-day note queued in PR #3060): the two-user staging harness now covers CME and On Call writes, not only reads. PR #3021 had added a read-only probe (scripts/lib/cross-tenant-records-probe.ts) using records user A already owns. The new scripts/lib/cross-tenant-write-probe.ts has user A create disposable records through the real API and then proves: user B cannot list, archive, mark transcribed, fully edit or delete A's CME entry, and A's entry is unchanged afterwards (CPD year 2000, created only when A has none, removed in cleanup); a non-member cannot read A's On Call service, mint an invitation, add a site or change a member; an invitation A issues lets B join and read, while B as a plain member still cannot invite or promote itself; after A revokes B, B loses access and the used invitation cannot bring B back. tests/cross-tenant-write-probe.test.ts runs it against a fake API and against seven leaking variants, each failing on the expected boundary. The same PR adds scripts/lib/cross-tenant-cme-evidence-probe.ts (user B gets 404 on A's evidence list, download and delete, and A's marker never appears in B's CPD export) and scripts/lib/cross-tenant-oncall-content-probe.ts (content writes, member roles, report visibility, orientation and revocation stay within the service), each with offline leak-variant tests. STILL NOT COVERED: concurrency races (revocation during an in-flight read) and evidence upload plus its signed download URL (the harness reports upload as not exercised). NOT YET RUN against staging: dispatching staging-tenancy.yml is provider-backed and needs the owner's approval. PRIOR DETAIL: Verified offline: scripts/test-cross-tenant-staging.ts exercises /api/answer, /api/documents, /api/health, /api/search and /api/search/universal and has zero CME or On Call references. Unit-level root-bundle isolation tests exist (cme-root-bundle-isolation, on-call-root-bundle-isolation) but are not endpoint attacks. Next action: extend the existing two-user harness to CME records/evidence/storage signed URLs and On Call services/invitations/membership/reports/orientation, including revocation and concurrency races. Staging execution is provider-backed and needs explicit approval. The label cap (strongSupportLabelCapped) is computed on the server when the answer is projected for the client, and the client payload carries only the boolean, not supportedClaims or evidenceAssessments, so the browser cannot recompute it. Server-side cached answers are re-projected on every response, so they pick up the rule (read from the answer routes, not tested). Not checked: whether any other store (saved or exported answers) keeps a client payload. | #H69FNZ <!-- issue-ulid:01M3GND2FBH69FNZ9NC7EK5XWT --> | P2 | issue | The search 'rerank' timing is mostly database time, so the 4.1 s blamed on re-ranking in #T9PRB9 is really database round trips | src/lib/rag/rag.ts starts rerankStartedAt at line 1453 and adds the elapsed time at 1484 and 1531, and src/lib/rag/rag-hydration.ts:137 does the same. Those windows include metadata hydration, memory-card hydration and visual-evidence hydration, which are Supabase round trips (Singapore to Sydney). The deterministic second-stage rerank itself is in-process arithmetic, and the semantic reranker is off by default (RAG_SEMANTIC_RERANK_ENABLED=false). So in the #T9PRB9 probe (search 9.2 s = rpc 4.2 s + rerank 4.1 s), nearly all search time is database work. Consequence: #CWWH2E (27 to 45 round trips per search, currently P3) is the first latency lever, not reranking. Also note: the 1.4 s rate-limit figure in #T9PRB9 predates 5c1ac4f87 (2026-09-26, one-round-trip anonymous rate limit), which has not been re-measured live. Next action: split rerank_latency_ms so hydration round trips are timed separately from ranking. This is RAG telemetry on a protected surface: flag before editing and write a RAG impact line (no ordering change expected). Then re-run scripts/probe-answer-timing.mjs from Perth (provider-backed, owner approval) to confirm. | Latency, network, Railway and wiring review 2026-09-27 (Claude Code session claude/repo-latency-network-review-zcqd5c), offline code read against main 7f8312f2c; no provider access | 2026-09-27 |

| #FZP7VW <!-- issue-ulid:01M3GND2NVFZP7VWK32ZS8QSZP --> | P2 | issue | Live search's query embedding can wait 45 s and retry twice, the same 45 s the browser allows, so a slow OpenAI reply fails the search instead of falling back to keyword results | embedTextWithTelemetry (src/lib/openai.ts) uses requestOptions with no per-call timeout, so it takes OPENAI_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS (45000, src/lib/env.ts:172) and OPENAI_MAX_RETRIES (2) via defaultMaxRetries (openai.ts:189). Callers on the interactive path (src/lib/rag/rag.ts around 1364 and 1703, rag-governed-search.ts:120) pass only the request signal. The browser search budget is searchRequestTimeoutMs = 45_000 (src/components/clinical-dashboard/search-utils.ts:423), so one slow embedding call always exhausts the user's whole budget. Next action: give the interactive query embedding its own short deadline (a few seconds, no retry) and let search continue on the lexical/text path when it expires, with telemetry. The caller side is a RAG-protected surface: flag before editing; a fallback-path change may need a canary pair. | Latency, network, Railway and wiring review 2026-09-27 (Claude Code session claude/repo-latency-network-review-zcqd5c), offline code read against main 7f8312f2c; no provider access | 2026-09-27 |
| #1DYJJQ <!-- issue-ulid:01M3GND2TK1DYJJQ741HPX5VME --> | P2 | issue | Most server-side Supabase calls have no deadline, so a stalled database request can hang for minutes on the single app replica | The service-role client (src/lib/supabase/admin.ts:16) is created without a global fetch timeout, and only 14 non-test files in src/ apply .abortSignal() to queries (the readiness probe in src/lib/supabase/health.ts does). Everything else relies on undici's default header/body timeouts (about 300 s). That includes the durable rate-limit RPCs every answer passes through (src/lib/api-rate-limit.ts:300, 360, 424). Search is bounded only by the client disconnecting at 45 s; other routes have no bound. Next action: add a default per-request deadline for server Supabase calls (for example a global fetch wrapper with AbortSignal.timeout, overridable for known long operations such as ingestion and exports), classify the expiry as unavailable, and cover it with a unit test. Check the ingestion worker's long jobs are exempt before rollout. | Latency, network, Railway and wiring review 2026-09-27 (Claude Code session claude/repo-latency-network-review-zcqd5c), offline code read against main 7f8312f2c; no provider access | 2026-09-27 |

Thread closure is fail-closed: a fixed reply must name its 40-character pushed commit SHA, and the workflow resolves the thread only when that SHA is the pull-request head. No-code dispositions carry a distinct marker, while missing, conflicting, or unlanded result claims leave the thread open with a visible workflow failure. | #125 | issue | `ui-therapy-nav-scroll.spec.ts` cites a spec file that does not exist | Resolved 2026-07-30. The Therapy navigation comment now points to the live `ui-mode-nav-density.spec.ts` coverage instead of the deleted `mode-nav-bar-anchoring.spec.ts` file. | 2026-07-30 |
| #095 | issue | `PR required` reports failure for concurrency-cancelled jobs | RESOLVED 2026-07-30. The `pr-required` aggregate now distinguishes a cancelled job from a failed one. `require_success` / `require_skipped_or_success` are thin wrappers over one `record()` collector that reads **each job's own `result`** and appends to a `failures` or `cancellations` array; both arrays are filled before anything is reported. **Genuine failures win:** every failure is emitted as its own `::error::` and a concurrent cancellation is demoted to a `::warning::`, so a run that is cancelled AND broken cannot read as an excuse (refinement reported by Codex on PR #1409). Cancelled with nothing failing stays **RED**, and the message states the two possibilities rather than asserting supersession: it points the reader at a newer `PR required` run on the PR's current head SHA, and says that if there is none the run was cancelled by hand and must be re-run rather than merged past. It deliberately does NOT read the workflow-level cancelled status function — an earlier revision passed that through an `env:` value, which is invalid because GitHub allows those functions only in `if:` conditions, so the whole file failed to parse, ran as `.github/workflows/ci.yml` instead of `CI`, and created zero jobs; valid YAML but invalid Actions schema, so prettier and every local gate passed it. **The tempting fix was rejected as unsafe:** treating `cancelled` as neutral, or skipping the aggregate via a not-cancelled condition, would make the red disappear, but GitHub counts a SKIPPED required check as PASSING, so a hand-cancelled run on the current head would become mergeable with nothing verified — `if: always()` is therefore deliberate. Guarded by ten cases in `tests/ci-cache-safety.test.ts` that EXECUTE the extracted aggregate script under synthetic job results rather than grepping the YAML. **Corrected in review (Codex, PR #1428):** the first version of this record described a shared `cancelled_error` helper that never existed and claimed the error names the newest run; both were carried over from an obsolete sentence in the open row and neither matched the shipped implementation. Source: PR #1316 runs 30340972329 / 30341225585; PR #1409 | 2026-07-30 |
| #096 | task | PR #1316 review follow-ups — adoption-gate coverage closed | RESOLVED 2026-07-30. Every sub-item is dispositioned. The band adoption gate's root-path gap closed on PR #1394 — root and href-less modes now resolve to `src/app/(search-app)/page.tsx` — and closing it surfaced two further defects in the same gate the original finding did not name: the hand-rolled walk was capped at two import hops where the root route's real chain is four, and it followed neither `layout.tsx` (where that route's band actually comes from) nor `dynamic(() => import(...))` (how the dashboard code-splits its mode workspaces). All three were fixed together with a bounded BFS, each verified load-bearing by reverting it and watching the gate fail. Four findings — favourites hub counts, the document-search status derivation, the 401 session-expiry path, the record-path duplicate notice — were already fixed independently. The Therapy Compass retry-waiter finding was corrected to NOT a live defect: `retryWaitersRef` is genuinely unscoped but no caller observes it. The seven Codex follow-up SHAs remain unreachable and were never pushed; the PR #1316 review threads are the durable source. Archived by the 2026-07-30 triage pass. Source: PR #1316 review sweep | 2026-07-30 |
| #104 | rec | Worker's triple image read is deliberate, not debt | NOT DEBT — archived so a fourth audit does not re-file it. The 2026-07-28 latency audit listed L4-2 (`worker/main.ts` reads each extracted image up to 3x per document — hash, caption on cache miss, upload) as "CONFIRMED with no fix evidence", carried forward from the 2026-07-01 audit's L11. **That was wrong.** The 2026-07-01 disposition table already recorded it as a deliberate peak-memory trade-off, and the rationale is documented in place at `worker/main.ts:866-869`: holding every extracted image Buffer for a document with hundreds of multi-MB page images would multiply the worker's peak memory, and disk I/O is the cheaper resource for a background pipeline. The three reads (`:872`, `:1034`, `:1129`) are real but accepted. This row carried no next action, so it is archived rather than left open — revisit only if ingestion throughput becomes a measured complaint AND a bounded-buffer design is proposed. **Stop:** do not re-file this as debt. Source: `docs/audit/repo-audit-2026-07-01.md` L11 + disposition table | 2026-07-30 |
| #109 | issue | Remote sessions clone shallow, invalidating branch analysis | RESOLVED 2026-07-29, hardened in review. `scripts/sweep-branch-ledger.mjs` refuses outright on a shallow clone via the exported `shallowCloneRefusal`, printing no inventory and exiting 1 in both text and `--json` mode, before the fetch and before any branch maths; `docs/branch-cleanup-guide.md` §Safety Rules gains the `is-shallow-repository` precondition ahead of its numbered steps. An indeterminate result (empty output from a swallowed `git` failure) is refused as its own failure, and the same guard had to move OUT of the reconciliation preflight CLI and INTO the exported `collectReconciliationState`, because `buildReconciliationEvidencePack` calls the collector directly and stamped `status: complete` — in a `--depth 1` clone the guarded CLI exited 1 while the evidence-pack command exited 0 and persisted shallow ahead/behind as completed evidence. The collector now throws `UnverifiedHistoryError` so every current and future caller fails closed by default. Review found two further routes to a false-empty inventory from half-checked refspecs; coverage requires exactly `refs/heads/*` to `refs/remotes/origin/*`. Proven against real `--depth 1` clones in `tests/repo-hygiene.test.ts`, `tests/reconciliation-preflight.test.ts` and `tests/reconciliation-evidence-pack.test.ts`. Source: session 2026-07-29 | 2026-07-29 |
| #115 | rec | Band adoption gate treats a discovered import as rendered | RESOLVED 2026-07-30 by redesign rather than another patch. `tests/search-results-band-adoption.test.ts` no longer asks whether a file mentions the band but whether anything the route actually mounts reaches it: it parses each module with `@babel/parser` into a small graph — exported name to local declaration, local to the identifiers its body references, and which locals render the band — then walks from the route's default export, carrying at each hop the set of exports the importer mounts. A static `import { X }` is followed only when `X` is reachable from a mounted declaration; `dynamic(() => import(...).then((m) => m.Named))` follows only that binding; a bare side-effect import is not followed at all; `export *` never supplies a default. **Why redesign:** six false greens were reported in one day, all one defect — presence is not reach — and two of the six had been introduced by an earlier patch to the same walker. Verified: all five production search routes still reach the band, gutting `services/page.tsx` and `tools/page.tsx` to `<div />` reports an orphan each, and fourteen temp-dir fixtures cover both directions with two mutation-confirmed. Accepted residual: reachability is per module and control flow inside a mounted declaration is not modelled. Source: PR #1400 | 2026-07-30 |

Root cause closed too: `npm run ledger:lookup` / `ledger:append` (`scripts/branch-review-ledger.mjs`) replace hand-written rows, and `check:branch-review-ledger` now fails on mojibake, cell width, heading records, impossible dates, table gaps, and (from 2026-07-29) unresolvable HEADs and near-duplicates. | 2026-07-28 |
| #097 | issue | Gitleaks reports a false red when the PR head moves mid-run | RESOLVED 2026-07-30: Secret Scan checks out the event head SHA and runs `scripts/run-gitleaks-pinned.mjs` against the immutable event base..head range (no mid-run PR commits API re-query). | 2026-07-30 |
| #111 | issue | `ui-overlap` phone-inset case still flakes under load | RESOLVED 2026-07-29 (PR #1391). The inset measurement now retries inside `expect(async () => …).toPass({ timeout: 15_000 })` (`ui-overlap.spec.ts:159`) — the same retry-the-measurement shape PR #1375 applied to the eight overlap cases. The 2px symmetry tolerance and the assertions themselves are byte-for-byte unchanged, so a genuinely asymmetric header still fails once the retry budget is spent; only a transient mid-remount sample settles. Full spec green across 4 consecutive runs (14 passed each). Do not relax the tolerance if this recurs — the assertion is the point; make the measurement robust instead. Source: PR #1375 flake triage; session 2026-07-29 | 2026-07-29 |
| #112 | issue | `issues:next-id` has no concurrency protection | RESOLVED 2026-07-30. `npm run check:outstanding-issues` now gates this file, in `verify:cheap` and in the `static-pr` CI job (the gate-manifest check refuses a local gate that CI does not run). It fails on a duplicate id, an id present in both tables, a marker at or below the highest id, a malformed row, and a missing heading or marker — so every shape the 2026-07-29 triple collision took is now a red gate rather than a silent row loss. Verified by replaying that collision against the real file: two rows claiming `#110` produced "#110 appears 2 times (lines 151, 164)", and a lost marker bump produced "issues:next-id=113 is not above the highest id #114". The checker honours `\|` escapes — its first run against the live file flagged row #042, which is correctly escaped, and a gate with false positives is a gate people switch off. NOT fixed: the underlying race. Ids are still allocated by read-modify-write with no lock, and this file still has no `merge=union` driver; what changed is that a collision can no longer land silently. Residual tracked as open `#156` (do not reopen this archive row). Source: session 2026-07-29 PR sweep; PR #1391 conflict resolution; `.gitattributes` | 2026-07-30 |
| #113 | issue | `ModeNav` clips its labels at every phone width on `main` | RESOLVED 2026-07-30 (PR #1405, landed on `main` as `020c1260`). The two lower density bands used `grid-auto-columns: 1fr`, so the widest slot set every slot's width and the label's `truncate` hid the shortfall — nothing overflowed and nothing failed, the word was simply gone. `display: flex` at every band removes the mode rather than retuning it; thresholds went `16/26/34rem` -> `22/33/42rem` against remeasured intrinsic widths (471px for four labels, not the 394px originally budgeted). `tests/ui-mode-nav-density.spec.ts` asserts at both band boundaries and one pixel either side, registered in BOTH Playwright allowlists. **Carry-forward:** the ~8% threshold headroom is not padding — a measured-plus-one-pixel attempt (21/31rem) passed locally and failed CI by exactly 1px, because the variable face rasterises wider on `ubuntu-24.04`. A threshold calibrated to one machine's font metrics is calibrated to nothing. | 2026-07-30 |
| #114 | issue | The live Web-Vitals instrument cannot measure its own noise | RESOLVED 2026-07-30 (PR #1407, landed as `a73db460`). Each route/strategy cell is measured `SAMPLES` times (default 3), the MEDIAN is graded, and a cell whose samples straddle a threshold is reported as incomplete evidence rather than resolved either way — so #017's "evidence is too noisy" stop rule can finally fire. Mid-run Chrome drift disqualifies the verdict. **Second defect found in review and fixed in the same PR:** `samples` is a free-text `workflow_dispatch` input, so `samples=1` reached the grader as a valid integer and reproduced the exact single-run matrix this work removes — verified against the pre-fix logic, it reported 0 breaches, 0 incomplete and printed the bold "Every mobile route is within" verdict. Now refused in three places (`main()` before reading any report, `renderTable`'s disqualifier list, and the workflow before spending a live measurement pass). | 2026-07-30 |
| #120 | issue | `verify:phone-chrome` exits 0 while reporting failed browser tests | RESOLVED 2026-07-30 — **closed as not reproducible, not as fixed.** The premise is contradicted by a reproduction of its own failure condition. **Evidence, `main` at `fcd9041`:** the container ships Chromium build `1194` while the repo wants `1234`, so `npm run verify:phone-chrome -- --files src/components/clinical-dashboard/mobile-composer-reserve.ts` reproduced the identical shape — the same `13 failed`, every one at `browserType.launch: Executable doesn't exist at /opt/pw-browsers/chromium_headless_shell-1234/…` — and the captured status was **`OVERALL_EXIT=1`**, not 0. The gate stopped at the `focused-browser` stage and never reached `full-ui`, which is correct; the three stages ahead of it were genuinely green (`Test Files 9 passed (9)`, `Tests 105 passed (105)`). **The code path agrees:** status propagates at `scripts/verify-phone-chrome.mjs:72-73` and `scripts/run-playwright.mjs:271-273`, and `childProcessExitCode` returns `1` when `status` is not an integer, so a launch error cannot read as 0. Neither file, nor `scripts/child-process-result.mjs`, has changed since `7581cfc`, which predates this row — so "fixed in the meantime" is ruled out and the original exit-0 reading is what is in doubt. **Leading hypothesis, explicitly unverified:** the 0 was read through a pipe, since `npm run verify:phone-chrome` piped to `tail` reports `tail`'s status rather than the gate's. The original command line was not recorded, so this cannot be confirmed. **Not covered by this reproduction:** the zero-selected-test case (a `--grep` matching nothing) was never exercised — only the launch-failure case this row describes. **If it recurs:** capture the exact command line and `echo $?` with no pipe before re-filing. | 2026-07-30 |
| #122 | issue | `ci/circleci: verify` fails on every branch and its log needs operator access | RESOLVED 2026-07-30 — moot: the status cannot mislead because the provider is gone from the repository. The row's own outcome allowed either branch ("trustworthy signal again, **or** it stops reporting"); the second is what happened, so no operator log read is needed and the **Next** step is withdrawn. **Evidence:** `.circleci/config.yml` was deleted by `9779828` ("chore: remove CircleCI from repo", PR #1412), and `ls -d .circleci` on `main` at `c5c1a86` returns `No such file or directory`. Confirmed live rather than only from the tree: PR #1452's head reported **21 check runs and not one named `ci/circleci: verify`**, so the status no longer reports on new PRs at all. The unverified quota hypothesis is retired unproven — it was never confirmable without a CircleCI token, and there is now nothing left to confirm it against. One residual was found and fixed in the same change: `AGENTS.md` still cited `ci/circleci: verify` as a check that fails on unformatted files, which would have sent a future session chasing a check that can never report again. | 2026-07-30 |

This branch also retains scoped `prettier-ignore` markers and a guard that rejects padded or unprotected tables.
